# UAM Alpha-2 performance and parity plan

## Summary

UAM can reach Codex-like responsiveness without replacing CEF. The earlier Alpha-3 performance
measurement reached 8.9 ms input-to-paint p95 under streaming, 23.0 MiB renderer heap, and
368.2 MiB process-tree RSS. That work has continued into the current Alpha-5 source. The shortest
route is to checkpoint it, then close four product gaps:
native Codex steering, provider-aware review, side chat, and dependable browser control.

OpenCode cannot currently match Codex's native in-turn steering. UAM should expose Queue and
Interrupt & send for OpenCode, not label interrupt plus restart as steering.

Current implementation hold: no new features. Only the bug-fix and measured performance sections are active.

## Baseline and version correction

| Item | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Daily-driver build | Installed `4.9.0-alpha-2` |
| Current local candidate | Built and tested performance source `4.9.0-alpha-5` |
| Current dirty source labels | `4.8.0-alpha-6` in several files |
| Alpha-9 | Not found locally or in git history |

Before further release builds, make one root version value drive the native binary, React metadata,
About UI, packaging, runner compatibility, and CI assertions. Do not guess Alpha-9.

## Performance comparison

The old live sample is useful for diagnosis, not release acceptance, because the two apps had
different uptime and open work.

| Live observation | UAM Alpha-2 | Codex | Meaning |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Shell CPU | 204.7 to 245.8% | 37.6 to 47.8% | UAM's own UI process was the primary CPU problem. |
| Shell memory | 748.0 to 1154.7 MiB | 524.5 to 833.4 MiB | Long-lived UAM renderer growth needs a soak test. |
| Whole-tree processes | 70 | 29 | UAM retains many chat-specific provider processes. |
| Whole-tree memory | 1190.6 to 1616.5 MiB | 924.6 to 1295.4 MiB | Fix renderer churn first, then remeasure provider lifetime. |

CEF is about 75% of UAM's application bundle, but Codex also bundles Chromium and has a larger total
application. Bundle size and UI responsiveness are separate problems.

The measured performance worktree already contains the changes worth keeping:

- `27600b8b`: stop continuous status-dot repaint. Idle tree CPU fell from 33.57% to 0.25% median.
- `9bbd93d5`: cache parsed chat view modes. Renderer benchmark improved 97.87% overall.
- `064df80f`: skip empty attachment conversion. Append work improved 36.53%.
- `f7678cd7`: fast-path active stream reconciliation. Streaming work improved 90.58%.
- `8c200c7a` and `3e114516`: omit empty collections. Message heap improved 37.15% and sanitizer peak heap improved 50.76%.
- `84f02234`: spare-renderer experiment. Do not land it. `bb102fd4` reverted it as unstable.

The proven commits are already on the Alpha-5 performance branch. Preserve their small boundaries and
checkpoint the remaining Alpha-5 working state before cross-branch integration.

Work started on Alpha-5 on 2026-09-03:

- `d4af0d1b`: keep the parsed pane-mode cache consistent when persistence fails.
- `9f7ed071`: remove all continuously running CSS repaint loops while keeping static state indicators.
- Verification: 637 frontend tests, 6 native test targets, production build, and strict code-sign check pass.
- Safe idle sample: median CPU was 0.1% across 20 samples; the final five samples used about 425 to 434 MiB RSS.

## Required changes

### P0: Version truth and repeatable measurement

1. Add one canonical version value and derive all build and CI checks from it.
2. Keep an Alpha-2 result fixture for 1, 4, and 9 panes, a 2,000-message chat, idle, eight stream
   chunks, and a 30-minute soak.
3. Measure fresh launches with the same data, pane count, uptime, and provider state. Record UI
   latency separately from provider network latency.

Acceptance: no conflicting product versions, input-to-paint p95 at or below 16.7 ms, settled idle CPU
at or below 1%, renderer heap at or below 30 MiB under the fixed stream case, and no post-warmup RSS
growth above 10% in the soak.

### P0: Complete the existing performance integration

Retain the measured commits already in Alpha-5. Checkpoint its still-uncommitted draft debounce,
turn-identity, runtime, and bug-fix work before broader integration. Measure each new logical commit.
Remove or revert a change that misses its gate.

Do not add process pooling. If provider processes remain material after the UI fix, retire only idle,
invisible sessions after a small fixed timeout and resume them through the existing session ID.

### P0: Native steering with honest provider controls

Codex:

- Add the App Server `turn/steer` request and response path.
- Send `threadId`, new input, and `expectedTurnId` while the matching turn is active.
- Do not send `turn/interrupt` when steering succeeds.
- If the turn ended or the ID is stale, queue the input as the next normal turn. Never cancel a newer turn.

OpenCode:

- Replace the misleading Steer now action with Queue and Interrupt & send.
- Keep generic interrupt plus send as an explicit user choice.
- Add native steering later only when OpenCode exposes a documented protocol operation.

Checks: request-shape unit test, same-turn state test, stale-turn race test, no-interrupt test, and UI
capability-label test.

### P1: Browser use

First, pass UAM's enabled MCP server configuration into the Codex process using scoped command-line
configuration. Do not write to the user's global Codex configuration. Pin the Playwright MCP package
version instead of using `@latest`.

Then create three static browser-pane mocks and stop for selection. After selection, build one
disposable prototype to prove navigation, screenshot capture, focus, clipboard, localhost access,
and authenticated tool control in a second isolated CEF context. Do not enable global unauthenticated
CDP access.

The MVP needs URL controls, back, forward, reload, localhost shortcuts, screenshot, and Attach to
chat. It does not need browser tabs, bookmarks, extensions, history sync, or a download manager.

### P1: Side chat

Create three static mocks and stop for selection. The implementation should reuse an ordinary child
chat with `parent_chat_id`, existing provider and workspace inheritance, and the current split-pane
system. Closing the side pane must not delete the chat. The child must survive restart and be openable
as a normal pane.

Do not add a second chat store, transcript format, or orchestration layer.

### P1: AI code review

Add one Review with provider action to the existing Review changes card.

- Codex uses native `review/start` on the current thread, starting with uncommitted changes.
- OpenCode uses one stored, provider-neutral review prompt over UAM's existing diff data.
- Findings render as a normal assistant turn and link path and line references to the existing diff dialog.

Do not build a new VCS panel or detached review workflow. Checks cover no working-tree mutation,
correct path and line navigation, cancellation, retry, and provider preservation.

### P1 dependency: Computer Use reliability

The existing bug-swarm owns implementation. This plan depends on its findings:

1. Give the helper a stable signing identity so macOS TCC approval survives rebuilds.
2. Report OS-ready only after the accessibility permission preflight succeeds.
3. Replace the full CEF-linked companion with a minimal dedicated helper.
4. Surface exact bridge failures in the current modal.
5. Correct the app named in denial instructions.
6. Validate the helper independently in CI.
7. Bound every `window.cefQuery` wait and make the developer launcher identity explicit.

Browser MCP work can proceed independently, but general Computer Use parity is not complete until
these P1 and P2 defects are closed. Source: [Computer Use bug-swarm report](./computer-use-bug-swarm/report.md).

## Safe implementation sequence

Each row is one small local commit unless two files are inseparable. Run the focused check before
committing and stage explicit paths only.

| Order | Commit intent | Minimum check |
| ---: | --- | --- |
| 1 | Make product version single-source | Version contract test and both builds |
| 2 | Freeze the controlled Alpha-2 benchmark | Benchmark self-check |
| 3 | Land proven performance commits in their existing order | Frontend tests, native focused tests, benchmark gate |
| 4 | Complete Computer Use fixes in its owner chat | Helper signing, permission, bridge, and packaging checks |
| 5 | Add Codex `turn/steer` protocol support | Focused ACP tests |
| 6 | Add stale-turn fallback and provider-specific labels | ACP and React tests |
| 7 | Inject pinned browser MCP config into Codex launch | Launch-argument tests |
| 8 | Add Codex and OpenCode review actions | Review and no-mutation tests |
| 9 | Produce side-chat mocks, then implement the selected reuse path | Persistence and pane tests |
| 10 | Produce browser mocks and control prototype, then implement the selected pane | Focus, permission, screenshot, and local-page checks |
| 11 | Run macOS and Windows release-candidate benchmarks | Full test, build, soak, and signed-package checks |

## Explicitly deferred

- Renderer migration. Reconsider only for a hard package-size target or a requirement to remove CEF
  patching and sandbox debt. Prototype WKWebView on macOS and WebView2 on Windows before any rewrite.
- Shared provider server or process pool. Add only if post-fix profiling proves idle retirement is
  insufficient.
- OpenCode native steer emulation. Interrupt plus restart is not steering.
- Five-provider handoff and attempt comparison, as requested.

### Model catalog stability, performance, and error dismissal

- Reproduced a matching model/list error failing a separate active Codex turn. The error now remains
  a catalog refresh failure: current prompt/processing state survives, the pending refresh clears,
  and the last successful model cache remains. Background-only discovery still stops. Focused tests
  and the full native core suite pass (70.60 seconds).
- MergeAcpModelArrays runs in state serialization and fallback catalog composition. Replaced its
  repeated vector membership scans with unordered_set and the existing InsertTrimmedNonEmptyString
  helper. Kept output order, fallback precedence, blank-ID rejection, and runtime deduplication.
  The focused behavioral check passes. Isolated O2 benchmark uses the actual before/after function
  bodies and existing inline helpers; median of seven samples, 100 merges per sample, half-overlap
  lists of the stated size: 20 models 27.40 -> 23.55 us; 500 models 1176.78 -> 430.72 us;
  1000 models 3417.00 -> 862.68 us. This is merge cost, not a measured whole-app speedup.
  Temporary reproducible benchmark: /tmp/uam-model-merge-bench.cpp.
- Settings model-refresh errors reuse Notice with provider/workspace/error identity. Dismissal hides
  the stale error, and a new failed refresh appears again. Updated existing test covers dismissal,
  retry, loading, and repeat failure. All 53 Settings tests pass.
- Official app-server documentation confirms model/list nextCursor; current handler ignores it.
  Pagination remains an open parity gap. Source: https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server#list-models-modellist
  Implement collection across pages without publishing a partial successful catalog, preserve cache
  on failure, and prevent repeated cursors from looping. This work is not yet implemented.

### Codex paginated model discovery

The previous open pagination gap is now implemented. model/list forwards opaque cursors unchanged
and stages parsed models until the final page. Duplicate/hidden entries are filtered across pages.
Only a complete nonempty catalog replaces available models and the persistent cache. JSON-RPC
errors, malformed pages, repeated cursors, and failed next-page writes discard staging and end the
catalog refresh. Failed writes also remove the unsent pending request. Transport errors retain the
shared write failure diagnostics; catalog errors do not fail an unrelated active turn.

Discovery staging resets on initialization and runtime reset. Publishing a catalog does not overwrite
the current model after a chat is ready, avoiding a late discovery reply undoing thread/model state.
The existing background-discovery process fixture now emits two pages and waits for the cursor
request before returning the second. It verifies the process stops only after the complete cache.
One additional focused test covers complete pages, duplicates, hidden entries, opaque cursor bytes,
second-page errors, repeated cursors, malformed data, and closed-pipe cleanup. Focused checks and
native rebuild pass. Full native regression passes; results are in /tmp/uam-pagination-native-suite.log.

Next frontier: continue connection and retention review plus the whole-tree cleanup/performance
and persistent-warning audit. OpenCode child-permission parity remains an upstream blocker; do not
claim all provider features or the entire audit complete. Frontend files are unchanged in this batch.

### Child-history polling and retention review

- requestChatMessagesFromCef launched its Promise with void and returned immediately, so the
  SubAgentHistory refreshing guard never waited for a real CEF response. Returning that existing
  Promise fixes the contract; the public action type now describes it. The existing unload test
  reproduced early completion and now verifies that completion waits while late unloaded data
  remains discarded.
- The history viewer now fetches one final UAM transcript after a running child completes or fails,
  including completion before the child chat ID resolves. Initially completed children do not cause
  an extra fetch. The existing polling test now uses deferred promises, verifies one outstanding
  periodic request, and covers final refresh on both successful and failed completion. Focused
  store/message checks pass before the additional failed-status case.
- Traced PTY byte handling: native recent output is capped at 256 KiB, frontend replay at 1 MiB,
  and xterm receives Uint8Array from the binary string. No UTF-8 decoding fix was warranted.
- Important separate parity gap: getChatMessages hydrates UAM storage; nonempty imported child
  transcripts do not trigger a provider-native refresh. openNativeSessionChat rejects remote
  workspaces. FindOrImportNativeSessionChatForOpen scans overlay/local UAM history. Do not claim
  complete live native child history from frontend polling tests. The parity guide now records this.
  Next: trace provider-native child import/sync for Codex and OpenCode, reusing targeted loaders and
  avoiding whole-history scans each second. This batch fixes response completion and final UAM
  snapshot retrieval only; broader provider child synchronization remains unresolved.

### Provider transcript parsing boundary

- OpenCode terminal history currently reads UAM storage, not a native snapshot. Its native reader is
  LoadRemoteOpenCodeTranscript, which runs `opencode export <id> --pure` through RunnerClient with
  a 30-second timeout and 4 MiB output cap. Local child export will need an asynchronous CEF path;
  invoking it synchronously in HandleOpenNativeSessionChat would block the GUI. No export process
  or native provider database was accessed during this inspection.
- Both ParseRemoteOpenCodeTranscript and ParseRemoteCodexTranscript could throw json type_error
  from nested string fields (roles, item types, tool IDs/status/commands). Existing outer shape
  validation did not cover these. Extended existing parser tests reproduced both exceptions.
  Added JSON-exception boundaries returning a failed, empty transcript with a clear error; valid
  prefixes are never returned as partial success. Tests cover six OpenCode and four Codex malformed
  field locations, including failure after valid messages. Focused parser tests and rebuild pass.
- Next performance candidate: AppShell statically imports SettingsModal and multiple closed memory
  dialogs. Check deferred loading against actual bundle sizes and unsaved-settings close behavior;
  do not change layout or raise the chunk warning threshold merely to silence the warning.

Native full suite passes; results in /tmp/uam-transcript-native-suite.log. Frontend unchanged in this batch.

### Native child rollback lifetime

- HandleOpenNativeSessionChat now uses its validated source_chat_id for resolved-mapping
  rollback. FindOrImportNativeSessionChatForOpen replaces app.chats while applying overrides,
  so the original source_chat pointer is invalid after import, including cached-child refresh.
- The changed CEF translation unit compiled successfully in Builds/tests; three existing
  child import/rollback/mapping checks pass. Those service tests do not exercise the CEF
  callback itself. git diff --check passes.
- Async OpenCode child export remains unimplemented. The existing async bridge requires a
  non-null callback on queue failure; any handler split must preserve exactly one completion,
  revalidate the source after export, and retain deletion/active-chat protections.

### Bridge exceptions and transcript filtering

- sendToCEF now converts synchronous dispatch/serialization exceptions into its existing
  failed response with the original requestId, honoring logFailures. Selection/rename callers
  use fulfilled failure responses for optimistic rollback and previously skipped rollback on
  rejected promises. Extended the existing rename/workspace-preservation test with a throwing
  bridge: red reproduced both stale optimistic name and unhandled rejection; green preserves
  the newer workspace and restores the old name. No speculative timeout/retry was added.
- FindOrImportNativeSessionChatForOpen filters candidate_chats with std::erase_if instead of
  copying every matching ChatSession and its transcript into a second vector. Order and
  workspace comparison semantics remain the same; no measured end-to-end speedup claimed.
- Full frontend suite: 694 pass (/tmp/uam-bridge-throw-tests-final.log). Isolated frontend build
  passes (/tmp/uam-bridge-throw-build.log). Rebuilt native suite passes in 75.81 seconds
  (/tmp/uam-history-filter-suite.log). git diff --check passes.
- Remaining history performance concern: ApplyLocalOverrides both overlays candidates and
  replaces app.chats; the child-opening caller snapshots/restores the full chat collection
  solely to use the overlay result. Separate those responsibilities only with behavior tests
  covering draft-link inference, active work, selection, and resolved-session mappings.

### Overlay without live-state replacement

- Extracted the shared overlay phase as private OverlayLocalHistory(const AppState&, ...),
  returning the local summaries needed by full merges. ApplyLocalOverrides still performs
  its normal complete merge. Child import directly overlays candidates and no longer copies,
  replaces, and restores all app.chats and resolved-session mappings. Parent transcript is
  now borrowed rather than copied; every use precedes any insertion into app.chats.
- With draft-link persistence disabled, skip draft-link inference and its unnecessary
  transcript hydration. The ordinary overlay still hydrates matching local messages when
  needed. The true-persistence path keeps its existing behavior.
- Existing child-reuse test now also checks parent selection remains unchanged. Full native
  suite passes in 72.57 seconds after extraction (/tmp/uam-overlay-suite.log). After the final
  draft-inference guard simplification, rebuild and all 33 relevant history/draft/link/import
  tests pass (/tmp/uam-overlay-focused.log). git diff --check passes.
- Supersedes the preceding snapshot/restore performance concern. No end-to-end timing claim.

- Added LoadLocalOpenCodeTranscript using existing ExecuteProviderWorkerInvocation with direct

- This API is not wired into GUI yet. Next: asynchronous CEF open path with UI-thread source

  reuse that behavior rather than duplicating it. RunAsyncCefQuery completion currently always
  forwards its callback, so nested handler callbacks would double-complete without a proper
  separation. UamQueryHandler is not refcounted; follow existing ownership/lifetime conventions.

### OpenCode child export integrated

- HandleOpenNativeSessionChat dispatches missing local OpenCode child opens and explicit
  refreshes through RunAsyncCefQuery. Worker owns a minimal child snapshot and performs export;
  UI completion checks the parent's provider, native session, workspace, host and folder before
  applying results. Existing cached selected opens remain synchronous.
- Extracted existing open/selection/persistence rollback body as FinishOpenNativeSessionChat
  returning AsyncCefResult; the shared async helper alone completes the callback. Synchronous
  dispatch sends that same result directly. No nested/double callback or null callback.
- FindOrImportNativeSessionChatForOpen accepts an optional exported snapshot, verifies identity
  and deletion records, and reuses existing overlay/collision/reuse behavior. Active pending
  calls or running CLI terminals keep their current child messages. A rejected refresh no
  longer writes its resolved-session mapping before validation.
- New service check covers initial import, refresh without duplicates, custom-title retention,
  parent selection, active unsaved work, wrong session/workspace, and tombstone rejection.
  Export reader/parser/process checks pass; full native suite passes in 73.56 seconds
  (/tmp/uam-export-integration-suite.log). Actual CEF handler translation unit compiles
  (/tmp/uam-export-handler-build-final.log). No running GUI/provider session was exercised.
- Updated docs/provider-runtime-parity.md: native local import implementation gap is closed,
  but live GUI/provider verification, periodic native updates, remote child access and the
  upstream OpenCode task-permission restriction remain. Next check async selection intent
  during slow child opening and isolated end-to-end callback behavior.

### Child-opening selection intent

- UI child opens now request selectChat:false and use the existing setActiveSession path only
  if their captured intentionalSelectionRevision is current. A newer child open increments
  that revision; clicking the already selected chat also counts as an intent and cancels a
  pending child selection. Embedded/read-only refreshes do not invalidate selection intent.
- Added refreshHistory to distinguish a cached open from explicit native refresh while keeping

### Runner result validation and atomic output parsing

- Request validates successful non-handshake result objects and the fields consumed by process
  and channel callers before they can update connection state. Rejects wrong string/boolean

  fields retain legacy defaults; a null running-process exitCode remains valid.
- Process/channel polling decode into temporary values, publishing only after successful decode.
  Reproduced invalid stderr overwriting running/stdout in the supplied result before returning false.
- Added an isolated test-executable framed bridge mode and one 13-case regression across process
  poll/start/write and channel open/poll. Red evidence: null result threw type_error.306; invalid
  stderr changed polled.running. Both focused tests now pass; native suite passes in 77.48 seconds.

### Async CEF handler lifetime

- OnBeforeClose cancels router queries and destroys UamQueryHandler. Queued completion closures
  captured raw this pointers without a lifetime check. Added one handler-owned lifetime token;
  shared async worker/callback tasks hold only weak references and drop queued work/completions
  after handler destruction. Already-running snapshot work can finish; its completion is dropped.
- All 21 async dispatch sites now pass the token, including the standalone worktree helper.
  This protects child/history, remote-host and other async callbacks at the shared boundary.
  CEF router source confirms cancellation detaches callback router references before handler reset.
- One runnable task-lifetime test verifies neither a queued worker nor queued completion executes
  after the owner token expires. Focused test and full native suite pass (75.91 seconds).
  Isolated app/core build and code-sign verification pass; subsequent edits only fixed trailing
  whitespace and updated handler documentation. git diff --check passes.
- Logs: /tmp/uam-async-lifetime-{focused,suite,build}.log. No live browser shutdown race exercised;
  desktop GUI verification remains outstanding. This does not cancel work already in progress.

### Remote browse completion cannot overwrite newer helper state
- Confirmed directory-browse completion wrote runner health by ID alone. A browse started while
  ready could complete during installation, overwrite installing, and cause installation's
  completion guard to reject its own result. Removed/reconfigured hosts also yielded stale listings.

  changed or removed hosts return a retryable 409 without saving or pushing obsolete health.
  ExecutionHost uses default equality so future fields participate without another field checklist.

  against installation, alias/directory/version changes, newer last-seen state, and unchanged hosts.

  and code-sign validation pass. Logs: /tmp/uam-host-health-*. GUI remains locked; no live SSH test.

### Markdown unmatched-bracket parsing avoids quadratic scans
- Confirmed inline link regex repeatedly rescanned suffixes for each unmatched `[`. Local Node
  timings for 10k/20k/40k brackets: 168.77/674.80/2695.78 ms. This parser runs on the UI thread.
- Exclude opening brackets from the simple link-label character class, in both token detection
  and extraction. Ordinary safe links/code/bold unchanged; unmatched brackets remain literal text.
- Repeated exact benchmark using the current production regex: 0.081/0.030/0.052 ms. These are
  isolated regex timings, not end-to-end UI/GPU measurements or proof about every Markdown shape.
- Extend existing safe-link rendering test with a 40k unmatched-bracket paragraph; preserve its
  exact text and verify adjacent valid/unsafe link handling. All 707 UI tests (43 files), isolated
  frontend build and diff whitespace check pass. Existing large-chunk warning remains.
  Logs: /tmp/uam-markdown-bracket-*. Remaining parser complexity cases require separate evidence.


### Markdown unfinished destinations avoid repeated suffix scans

  characters took 42.40/168.91/674.56 ms in the old regex.

  Final 707 UI tests (43 files), isolated frontend build and diff whitespace check pass. Existing
  large-chunk warning remains. Logs: /tmp/uam-markdown-destination-*. No new dependency.

### 61. Surface failed terminal input delivery
- CLIView discarded writeCliInput responses. Native PTY write failures normally push state, but bridge delivery errors had no visible feedback, leaving typed input silently lost.

### 62. Outstanding native terminal input acknowledgement defect
- Confirmed in HandleWriteCliInput (src/cef/terminal_handlers.cpp): nonempty data with a missing, stopped, or ShuttingDown terminal skips the write branch and falls through to cb->Success("{}"). CLIView's newly handled failure response therefore cannot cover these native states yet.
- Required behavior: empty input remains a harmless no-op; nonempty input to an unavailable terminal must fail with a clear error; live terminal input preserves raw bytes and existing transport-failure reporting.
- Verification gap: native core tests cover WriteToCliTerminal transport failure but do not invoke the CEF handler. A source-text assertion would not prove callback behavior. The next useful check is an isolated app bridge request for missing/stopped/shutting-down routing, followed by a live raw-input check. Mac lock was rechecked and still prevents the GUI tool from operating. No native handler change made without that check.
- CLI teardown inspection found existing attachment ownership, cancellation, listener disposal, observer disposal and late-start detach guards. No additional teardown change justified.

### 63. Reject input for unavailable native terminals
- Implemented the missing HandleWriteCliInput else branch: nonempty input for a missing, stopped or shutting-down terminal returns HTTP-style CEF failure 409 with restart guidance. Empty input still succeeds; live-terminal raw writes and existing transport-error handling are unchanged.
- This resolves the source defect recorded in section 62. The earlier decision to require live GUI access before this small guard was unnecessarily restrictive; made the correction while retaining the verification limit explicitly.
- Validation: isolated native application build and code-sign validation pass; CliWriteTransportFailureCannotRemainRunning passes; all nine CLIView tests pass, including failed-write error display/dismissal and raw-byte forwarding. These are surrounding runtime/UI checks, not execution of the CEF handler itself. Live callback checks for missing/stopped/shutting-down terminals remain outstanding because the Mac is locked. git diff --check clean.

### 64. Remove successful-keystroke diagnostic overhead
- HandleWriteCliInput logged every successful write. Each LogCliDiagnosticEvent invocation builds an ostringstream, performs three chat identity lookups via separate field helpers and writes to std::cerr.
- Keep the existing failure diagnostic inside the failed-write branch; remove the successful-write event. Turn-submission and lifecycle events remain. For 1,000 successful non-submit input callbacks, this removes 1,000 diagnostic records and 3,000 diagnostic chat lookups. This is an operation-count reduction, not a measured latency or disk-I/O claim.
- Validation: isolated application rebuild/code-sign validation passes; three existing native checks for diagnostics, lifecycle and transport failure pass. git diff --check clean. No deletion-only test added.

### 70. Drain final MCP output after local process exit
- RunLocalMcpRelay read one 16 KiB chunk and then exited immediately when its child stopped, discarding pipe-buffered response bytes. It now performs reads after observing exit and closes only once those reads yield no buffered output. No further input polling occurs once the process is known stopped.
- Astra extended the existing shared-service MCP integration fixture with a replacement local child emitting a 128 KiB JSON response then exiting. The old runner failed the complete-response assertion; rebuilt fixed runner passes it and the existing lost-ack retry check.
- Validation: isolated core, uam-runner and application builds pass; all six native CTest targets pass (71.42 seconds); git diff --check clean. Fixture uses a local fake SSH bridge, not a real host. Windows source shares the loop but real Windows execution remains unverified.
- Section 69 queued-input acknowledgement candidate remains open; no delivery-semantics change made without reproduction.

### 71. Acknowledge MCP channel input after pipe delivery
- Confirmed the section 69 candidate: WriteToStdioProcess only enqueues bytes, while the relay immediately acknowledged them. A blocked-reader fixture reproduced premature acknowledgement.
- Relay now retains a pending input cursor until the existing async writer has no pending bytes, then acknowledges it. It continues draining output while waiting and does not poll/requeue the same input. Existing FailureOrStall reports writer failure before acknowledgement. Applies to the shared macOS/Windows/Linux process fields; no new transport abstraction.
- Astra extended the existing shared-service fixture; parent revised its observation loops to steady-clock deadlines so they cannot accidentally exceed the writer's five-second stall deadline. Sends 512 KiB through the real runner channel to a blocked local reader, checks retained data, then releases it and checks exact bytes plus final acknowledgement.
- Final bounded fixture fails against the rebuilt old implementation at premature acknowledgement and passes with the fix. Isolated core/runner/application builds and all six CTest targets pass (74.78 seconds); final explicit chrono type annotations compile; git diff --check clean. CMake target is uam_runner, with output filename uam-runner. Do not use the output filename as the standalone rebuild target.
- Delivery boundary is the OS stdin pipe, not proof of MCP action completion. Exactly-once application execution across child crash/replacement remains unproven; do not infer it from this check.
- CUA getState now returned inventory without the former lock error. Actual isolated-app screenshot/interaction has not yet been retried. Existing isolated launch script/data fixture are available under /tmp; next GUI work must continue to protect the running daily-driver app.

### 101. Add private one-use remote terminal launch handoff

Leased channels advertise a capability, expire within60s, cap64 pending channels and64KiB combined payload, reject ordinary poll/ack/attach, and consume exactly once without retry. Fresh UUIDs must never be reused, including after expiry/cancel/uncertain replies. Pending leases prevent service shutdown; both platform dispatch paths serialize open against shutdown. Expired handoffs are logically invalid immediately and purged on subsequent runner activity, not a new reaper thread. No running provider exists for an abandoned unclaimed password.

Legacy `terminal SPEC` still rejects environment values. Shared terminal decoding removes duplicated Windows/POSIX parsing; embedded NULs and invalid environment names fail before execution. SSH command contains only a bounded channel ID/socket location, never provider launch bytes or credentials. No installed app or daily data touched.

### 174. Replayed steering rejection during remote recovery, reproduced

- Previous goal turn classified as progress: native steering anchor correction passed focused/full tests. This turn re-read current response dispatch, remote prompt persistence/replay, receipt clearing and stdout acknowledgment paths.
- Added focused regression CodexRemoteRecoveryDoesNotTreatUncorrelatedSteerRejectionAsTurnFailure in acp_session_tests.cpp. A replacement GUI attaches to an active remote Codex turn with no request map; replayed request17 rejection belongs to steering. Expected current turn remains processing. Build14376 terminal0; focused run terminal1 (/tmp/uam-recovery174-red.log). This is a deliberate RED regression, not a passing checkpoint. No product fix174 yet; do not package/commit this batch. All handles terminal.
- Root cause at acp_response_handlers.cpp HandleAcpResponse: unknown IDs while recovering are unconditionally classified as prompt responses. Request method/context is not durable. A generic error payload cannot distinguish turn/start from turn/steer, model/list or other requests; message-text matching and shape guesses would be incorrect fixes.
- Required integration: persist pending request identity/method and steering user/turn correlation before remote input writes, retain correlation beyond helper delivery receipt until provider response is durably consumed, and restore it before processing replayed stdout. Keep exact delivery IDs for deduplicated retransmission and support multiple outstanding steers. Existing single remote_prompt_delivery_payload is cleared at helper transport receipt and cannot represent concurrent steering. Existing remote output cursor save-before-ACK should own atomic response consumption. Account for old saved chats without correlation explicitly; never infer arbitrary errors as prompt failure.
- Preserve existing protocol behavior through provider interface; no provider-specific include in shared runtime/tests. ### Current scope: computer use only (user override)
- User replaced broader goals with computer-use accuracy. Do not stop/restart running app or sessions. Remove redundant UAM target popup in source, then investigate code. Goal tool could not replace unfinished blocked objective; this checkpoint records authoritative scope.
- Code finding: DescribeAxElement accepts any intersecting AX bounds without clipping; Act derives centre from full bounds and rejects it outside screenshot. Thus a valid listed elementId can itself cause reported coordinates-outside error. Fix visible intersection geometry, not arbitrary input clamping.
- Code finding: FindAxElement assigns IDs by a fresh traversal rather than retaining observation identity. Dynamic tree changes can select another element with the same ordinal. Validate stable element identity before action.
- Code finding: Act captures current_capture before input but only checks ok; neither pixels nor element identity are compared, and ExecuteAction receives m_lastCapture. Existing recapture cost does not prevent stale actions.
- Code finding: drag emits one endpoint drag event with no intermediate motion/duration, then immediate release. Complex drag/drop controls may not enter drag state reliably. Needs bounded timed movement and cancellation/release checks.
- DesktopPoint uses capture width/height to scale to desktop bounds plus origin; no obvious Retina/origin error in this formula from static inspection.
- User specifically reports target isolation failure and vertical cursor offset. ModernCapture does not set ignoreShadowsSingleWindow although SDK exposes it, while DesktopPoint maps whole screenshot to CGWindow bounds; shadow/framing can introduce coordinate offsets. Need explicit unframed capture and measured landmark verification.
- Window ExecuteAction deliberately FocusAxWindow/raises application; foreground control is implementation behavior, not isolated surface input. PID routing does not prevent focus changes. Clarify intended isolation before promising background-only operation.
- Cursor visual is 18x21 proportionally fitted image in NSRect(21,17,18,21), but hotspot hardcoded (39,30); verify against actual asset tip (asset viewed). Separate drawn hotspot from event coordinates; do not compensate event mapping with guessed offsets.
